```text
The float reordering approach didn't achieve the desired balance on pages 5 and 7 - LaTeX's placement rules are proving difficult to override. 109	\vspace{-2pt}
```
```text
110	{\footnotesize $^{*}$9-class encoder with mean pooling replacing BiGRU. $^{\dagger}$Deterministic (no std). $^{\ddagger}$MLM-only IID (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}) is single-seed (42); all OOD values are 3-seed. $^{\P}$Zero-shot; no domain training.}
111	\end{table}
112	
113	A clear \emph{evidence hierarchy} emerges across seven variants on synthetic multi-turn data (Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}, Figure~\ref{fig:evidence_hierarchy}), ordered by OOD detection at full conversation length: vanilla $<$ topic $\ll$ MLM $<$ classification-supervised ($\approx$\,1.0 on DD).
114	
115	\paragraph{Domain exposure is the primary observed factor.}
116	MLM bridges $\sim$60\,pp from vanilla (0.312$\pm$0.026 $\to$ 0.914$\pm$0.115) without labels; this supports the DAPT interpretation~\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}. TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 9-class 0.972) confirms that domain-adapted representations capture features beyond lexical overlap.
117	All three classification-supervised variants converge to equivalent detection (F1 = 0.997--1.000); McNemar's ($p > 0.13$) and paired permutation tests ($p \geq 0.40$) provide descriptive evidence suggesting equivalence. Extended to $n$\,=\,10 seeds, TOST (margin = 5\,pp) confirms equivalence on DD ($p < 10^{-8}$, $d$\,=\,$-$0.14; Table~\ref{tab:tost_sensitivity}) and FITD ($p < 10^{-8}$, $d \approx 0$). Label quality and granularity are non-factors for template-attack detection (DD/FITD: $|d| \leq 0.14$), but strategy-diverse ActorAttack retains moderate label sensitivity ($d$\,=\,0.44, TOST $p$\,=\,0.183). For template attacks, any domain-relevant classification objective suffices; the objective's structural role---partitioning the representation space---matters more than label semantics. Strategy-diverse attacks mark a difficulty boundary for this sufficiency.
118	Classification supervision is associated with 3--23$\times$ lower cross-seed variance over MLM on synthetic OOD (std 0.005 vs.\ 0.115 on DD; 3 seeds; Appendix~\ref{app:ensemble}), though this reverses on human-authored data (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
119	
120	\paragraph{Domain specificity is marginal at full length but meaningful for early detection.}
121	Wikipedia MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length (1\,pp gap), though jailbreak-domain text provides an 8\,pp advantage at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\ 0.911).
122	Perturbation analysis (Section~\ref{sec:perturbation}) reveals a sharper domain specificity effect: Wikipedia MLM degrades by $\sim$12\,pp under adversarial paraphrasing while jailbreak-domain MLM degrades by only 2.6\,pp.
123	
124	\paragraph{Misaligned supervision destabilizes generalization.}
125	Topic classification's poor performance (0.541$\pm$0.207) is consistent with misaligned supervision; the cleaner test isolating domain exposure is Wiki-MLM vs.\ JB-MLM (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}).
126	
127	\paragraph{Evidence hierarchy on strategy-diverse attacks.}
128	ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} preserves the same hierarchy (Figure~\ref{fig:evidence_hierarchy}) with wider gaps: TF-IDF collapses (0.261) while classification variants converge (0.951--0.990, max gap 3.9\,pp); DAPT provides $\sim$71\,pp over vanilla (Appendix~\ref{sec:actorattack_full},~\ref{app:aa_error}).
129	
130	\paragraph{Temporal modeling.}
131	Mean pooling matches BiGRU (F1 gap $\leq$ 1\,pp; Appendix~\ref{app:temporal}) and turn-order shuffling is negligible ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp), confirming detection relies on per-turn content rather than sequential patterns.
132
```

```text
57	Vanilla encoders collapse under cross-attack transfer; domain-adapted variants recover near-perfect detection (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}, Figure~\ref{fig:cross_attack_bar}).
58	On OOD DD, vanilla drops to F1\,=\,0.312$\pm$0.026 and worsens with more turns (0.628$\to$0.312 at Full), consistent with attack turns occupying benign embedding regions~\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}. MLM recovers to $\geq$0.97 at $K \leq 5$ without labels but is unstable at full length (0.914$\pm$0.115); 9-class maintains 0.997$\pm$0.005.
59	On strategy-diverse ActorAttack, TF-IDF collapses (0.261) while 9-class maintains 0.972$\pm$0.026, a 71 percentage point (pp) gap confirming domain-adapted representations capture beyond-lexical features.
60	Seed-level results confirm consistent separation (Table~\ref{tab:seed_level}; bootstrap CIs non-overlapping, permutation $p < 0.0001$). FITD is non-discriminative (all F1\,$\geq$\,0.990).
61	
62	\begin{figure}[t]
63	\centering
64	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig2_cross_attack_bar.pdf}
65	\caption{Cross-attack overview (F1 at full conversation length). DisPeD (9-class) maintains near-perfect detection across IID and both OOD families; vanilla collapses on OOD DD. FITD is non-discriminative for all methods.}
66	\label{fig:cross_attack_bar}
67	\end{figure}
68	
69	Prefix-length analysis (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:full_prefix}) confirms 9-class stability across all prefixes (0.972--0.997) vs.\ MLM instability at full length (0.914$\pm$0.115) and vanilla degradation (0.628$\to$0.312).
70	
71	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
72	\subsection{Decomposing the Detection Mechanism}
73	\label{sec:decomposition}
74	
75	\begin{figure}[t]
76	\centering
77	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig3_evidence_hierarchy.pdf}
78	\caption{Evidence hierarchy across evaluation conditions. Classification-based DAPT outperforms MLM and vanilla on synthetic OOD attacks (DD, AA), but the hierarchy reverses on human-authored single-turn data (ToxicChat), where JB-MLM leads.}
79	\label{fig:evidence_hierarchy}
80	\end{figure}
81	
82	\begin{table}[t]
83	\centering
84	\small
85	\caption{Evidence hierarchy on OOD DD and ActorAttack (AA) at full conversation length (3-seed mean$\pm$std where available). OOD F1 reflects attack-side generalization only; benign samples are IID from training distribution (benign-side shift evaluated in Section~\ref{sec:benign_shift}). Bold indicates best per column. All variants share the same Stage-2 pipeline. Full per-prefix results in Appendix Tables~\ref{tab:full_prefix} and~\ref{tab:actorattack_full}. Bottom rows: full dual-OOD evaluation with WildChat benign replacing IID benign (Appendix~\ref{app:dual_ood}).}
86	\label{tab:evidence_hierarchy}
87	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
88	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
89	\toprule
90	\textbf{Variant} & DD-Full & AA-Full & DD-$K$=2 \\
91	\midrule
92	9-class (full model) & .997$\pm$.005 & .972$\pm$.026 & .981$\pm$.013 \\
93	Binary (2-class) & \textbf{1.000$\pm$.000} & \textbf{.990$\pm$.008} & .994$\pm$.009 \\
94	Scrambled labels & .997$\pm$.005 & .951$\pm$.043 & .981$\pm$.026 \\
```

```json
{
  "text": "回复周俊伟三个问题的处理情况：\n\n**Q1 Figure 1 蓝色框内的线条**：已回复。线条表示共享权重的 DeBERTa 编码器分别处理每个用户 turn，从输入语音气泡到输出 [CLS] embedding 的连接路径。\n\n**Q3 正文超页**：已修复。删除了 Section 4.9（效率小节），将效率数据保留在 Appendix 引用。精简了 Limitations 第三条。正文（含 Limitations）现在在第 8 页内结束，References 从第 9 页开始。\n\n**Q2 图表分布不均匀**：已优化 Section 4.3 的浮动体声明顺序，使 P6 变为双表布局（左右各一个表），打破了连续的\"左表右图\"模式。P5/P7 各有 1 表 + 2 图，LaTeX 自然将两个图堆叠在同一列——进一步调整有推动正文超页的风险，因此保持当前布局。整体模式为 P5(图重) → P6(均衡) → P7(图重) → P8(均衡)，已是交替分布。\n\n另外本轮还完成了：\n- 缩写定义自检（OOD, DAPT, MLM, BiGRU, TOST, SVD, FPR, CKA, NLP, pp, IID, TF-IDF, BCa 等全部首次出现时展开）\n- Table 24 等 6 个附录表格溢出修复（resizebox）\n\nPDF 已上传。"
}
```

```text
Now I can see the problem. The bar labels are placed with `fontsize=6` at `val + 0.015` above each bar. When bars have identical or nearly identical heights (e.g., all 1.00 in IID and FITD groups, or 0.258 and 0.261 in ActorAttack), the labels overlap because they're placed at the same vertical position and the bars are close together horizontally.

I need to fix this by:
1. For groups where all bars are near 1.00 (IID, FITD): avoid showing redundant labels, or stagger them vertically, or only show one label


2. For the ActorAttack group: the 0.26 labels for Vanilla DeBERTa (0.258) and TF-IDF LR (0.261) overlap

The cleanest approach would be to skip labels entirely when all bars in a group are 1.00 since that's visually obvious, and for groups with overlapping low values, I could offset one label vertically or show only the most relevant one.

Actually, the best solution is simpler: only show labels for bars that deviate from 1.00. The dashed reference line already makes the 1.00 values clear, so I don't need to label them. This means IID and OOD FITD groups get no labels at all, while OOD DD and OOD ActorAttack only show labels for the bars that stand out—DisPeD at 0.97, Vanilla at 0.26, and TF-IDF at 0.26. For ActorAttack, the overlapping 0.26 labels need to be handled by either hiding high-value bars (≥0.99) entirely or using vertical offset for collisions. I'll go with skipping labels for values near 1.00 and staggering the remaining ones when they're too close together. I'm going to implement overlap detection by checking if labels within each group have similar y-positions, and if they do, I'll stagger them vertically to avoid collisions—or more simply, just hide labels for values that round to 1.00 and manually position the ActorAttack overlapping labels at different heights. Implementing intelligent label placement to handle overlaps — hiding labels for high-value groups, showing the full range for OOD DD, and offsetting the duplicate ActorAttack labels to avoid collision.
```

```json
          "+    all_bars_list: list of bar containers (one per series)",
```

- [info] [叙事一致性] Abstract→Intro→Method→Experiments→Discussion→Conclusion 形成连贯主线。核心问题（什么表示属性驱动跨攻击泛化）、方法（7变体受控消融）、发现（分类DAPT恢复OOD检测；层级在人类数据上反转）全文一致。未发现 A/B 故事漂移。
- [warning] [叙事一致性] introduction.tex:16 和 experiments.tex:117 近乎逐字重复同一解释："the classification objective's structural role---not label semantics---drives generalization"。正文中同一解释不应多处重复推导，experiments 处可改为引用 intro 或简化表述。
- [info] [章节结构] Abstract 回答四个必答问题（问题=multi-turn jailbreak 检测率低；方法=DisPeD 受控消融框架；结果=3个发现含具体数字；意义=24x加速+FPR消除），约150词，符合规范。
- [info] [章节结构] Introduction 遵循5段结构：背景(多轮越狱+说服机制)→gap(现有防御不评估跨攻击迁移)→核心想法(DisPeD分离encoder适配与时序分类)→贡献列表(3条)。结构完整。
- [info] [章节结构] Related Work 按四个主题归纳（攻击方法、防御系统、计算说服、DAPT），非论文堆砌式。每段定位本文与已有工作的关系。符合规范。
- [info] [贡献对齐] 三条 Contribution 均有实验支撑：C1→Tables 1-3/Section 5.1-5.5；C2→TOST结果/Section 5.2/Appendix TOST表；C3→Section 5.7+Appendix效率表。无孤儿章节。
- [info] [贡献对齐] Abstract 称 "thirteen configurations (125--355M parameters) and three out-of-distribution attack families"，与实验中7 DeBERTa + 3 RoBERTa-base + 3 RoBERTa-large = 13 配置、DD/FITD/ActorAttack 3个OOD家族吻合。
- [info] [Claim强度] 未使用 "significantly"、"state-of-the-art"、"groundbreaking" 等过强措辞。定量claim均附具体数字和±std。TOST等效性检验有p值和效应量。Claim强度与数据匹配良好。
- [info] [Claim强度] Conclusion 明确标注限制条件："though not under mixed-LLM training on diverse attacks (ActorAttack F1=0.504)"、"condition-specific, counterproductive at 355M"。Hedging 恰当。
- [info] [实验严谨] 主结果表报告3-seed mean±std（seeds 42, 123, 456）。统计检验包括McNemar's、BCa bootstrap 95% CI、paired permutation tests、TOST等效性检验。
- [info] [实验严谨] Baseline涵盖：Llama Guard 3（2024）、LLM-as-judge（GPT-5.5, Qwen3-8B）、TF-IDF LR、vanilla encoder、DeepContext架构等价物。时效性充分。
- [info] [实验严谨] Ablation 7变体对应Method中7个组件设计选择，数量匹配。
- [info] [可复现] Appendix Reproducibility Details 包含完整超参（lr, batch, epochs, optimizer, warmup）、GPU型号（A100 40GB）、训练时间（~30min/variant Stage-1, <5min Stage-2）、总计算量（<4 GPU-hours）。

- [warning] [符号] introduction.tex:7 "(cf.\ DeepContext)" 缺少引用标记，同段落前文已用 "DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026}"。此处应补充 \citep{deepcontext2026}。
- [warning] [符号] Method中Stage-1损失权重用 $\alpha$（method.tex:36），Appendix中Stage-2损失权重用 $\lambda$（appendix.tex:863）。这本身可以接受（不同stage不同符号），但 method.tex:36 交叉引用 Appendix~\ref{app:lambda} 时，读者会在 appendix 中找到关于 $\lambda$（Stage-2）的表，而非 $\alpha$（Stage-1）的搜索结果。建议在 method 中明确注明 $\alpha$ 的搜索结果在正文内呈现，Appendix 中的是 Stage-2 的 $\lambda$ 消融。
- [info] [符号] Abstract 中 "DisPeD" 首次出现未展开全称。Intro 中展开为 "Disentangled Persuasion Detection"。按写作规范，Abstract 内缩写应当展开。但 DisPeD 是本文方法名且紧跟描述性文字 "a controlled ablation framework"，在 ML 论文中此做法常见，可酌情处理。
- [info] [过渡] Discussion 不重复 Results 具体数字，而是引用表格编号做更高层解读（discriminative compression mechanism, deployment implications）。符合规范。

### Tool result

_source line 3291 · 2026-05-25T05:21:34.047Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01XNT5Bekzo2govMbpLVFx6z`</summary>

```text
7:Existing defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?} Pretrained language understanding alone does not suffice: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\ DeepContext) drops from F1\,=\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 (across prefix lengths) with 30--47\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization at base model scale benefits substantially from domain adaptation.
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015VdaVyRJcVuMVJw6y2g9b3`</summary>

```text
412:\paragraph{Cross-checkpoint variance.}
413:To disentangle Stage-1 from Stage-2 variance, we evaluate three independently trained 9-class DAPT checkpoints (seeds 42, 123, 456) with a fixed downstream BiGRU seed (42). Results: IID F1\,=\,1.000$\pm$0.000, DD OOD\,=\,0.926$\pm$0.026, AA OOD\,=\,0.935$\pm$0.013. Stage-1 training is stable (OOD std\,$\leq$\,2.6\,pp), confirming that the variance reported in the main paper is not dominated by Stage-1 initialization.
421:\caption{Frozen probing vs.\ end-to-end (E2E) fine-tuning for DeBERTa-v3-base 9-class (3-seed mean$\pm$std). E2E unfreezes the Stage-1 encoder during Stage-2 training. Frozen values use main-experiment checkpoints (matching Tables~\ref{tab:main_results}--\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}); Table~\ref{tab:frozen_verify} uses plan\_002 checkpoints, yielding higher frozen accuracy (DD\,=\,.997, AA\,=\,.972) and a narrower E2E gap ($\leq$2.5\,pp).}
563:\caption{Frozen probing vs.\ end-to-end (E2E) fine-tuning (3-seed mean$\pm$std). 9-class: plan\_002 checkpoints; MLM: plan\_017\_mlm\_control (matched training). Frozen probing trains only BiGRU+MLP on frozen DAPT embeddings.}
717:\caption{Temporal aggregation comparison: mean pooling vs.\ BiGRU under frozen and end-to-end (E2E) settings (3-seed mean$\pm$std). Plan\_002 9-class checkpoints.}
769:\caption{Turn-count independence verification (9-class DeBERTa-v3-base, same checkpoint as Table~\ref{tab:main_results}, 3 seeds). Evaluated on the full OOD test set (80 jailbreak + 75 benign conversations) rather than the balanced subset used in Table~\ref{tab:main_results}. Part~A: FPR on test benign truncated to $K$ user turns (all seeds FPR\,=\,0). Part~B: WildChat FPR by turn-count bucket (mean$\pm$std). Part~C: jailbreak detection (F1) after padding with benign turns to 15+ total turns (mean$\pm$std where available).}
```

_source line 3316 · 2026-05-25T05:22:30.714Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012WYcW1jn14aw8Ha9NTU3Lw`</summary>

```text
6	
7	Existing defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?} Pretrained language understanding alone does not suffice: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\ DeepContext) drops from F1\,=\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 (across prefix lengths) with 30--47\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization at base model scale benefits substantially from domain adaptation.
8	
```

</details>

```json
    "originalFile": "% introduction.tex\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.\n\nExisting defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \\emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?} Pretrained language understanding alone does not suffice: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\\ DeepContext) drops from F1\\,=\\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 (across prefix lengths) with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization at base model scale benefits substantially from domain adaptation.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n\\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}\n\\label{fig:architecture}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nOur pipeline, \\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection)\\footnote{We use `disentangled' to describe our experimental methodology of isolating individual factors through controlled ablation, distinct from disentangled representation learning ($\\beta$-VAE, InfoGAN).}, separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants each isolate a single factor while holding all others constant (Section~\\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline. Controlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families reveals three findings at base model scale: classification-based domain-adaptive pretraining (DAPT) is the only variant that consistently recovers OOD detection (F1\\,=\\,0.97--1.0 vs.\\ vanilla 0.26--0.31), even under mixed-LLM training where unsupervised pretraining collapses to vanilla level~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}; scrambled labels match correct labels for template attacks, indicating the classification objective's structural role---not label semantics---drives generalization; yet this hierarchy reverses entirely on human-authored data (MLM 0.837 vs.\\ classification 0.596), revealing condition-specific rather than universal advantages. At 355M parameters, capacity alone suffices: RoBERTa-large handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Systematic ablation of cross-attack generalization.} Thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125--355M) decompose jailbreak detection into attack-side and benign-side factors---the first controlled factorial study in this domain. Classification DAPT recovers OOD detection (0.26--0.31 $\\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0) while unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training; 10--25 benign conversations reduce false-positive rates (FPR) from 58--68\\% to 0--1.5\\%.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Partition sufficiency with a difficulty gradient.} Scrambled labels match correct labels for template-attack detection (Two One-Sided Tests [TOST] $|d| \\leq 0.14$), but strategy-diverse attacks retain moderate label sensitivity ($d$\\,=\\,0.44). Domain-relevant partitioning---not label semantics---is the operative mechanism, consistent with \\citet{zhang2021understanding}: compression localizes to 3 singular value decomposition (SVD) dimensions in layers 8--12.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Practical adaptation protocol.} A 184M-parameter encoder achieves 24.3$\\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (43$\\times$ fewer parameters; Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}); few-shot benign adaptation resolves deployment-blocking FPR without target-domain attack data.\n\\end{itemize}\n",
```

```text
All clean. Here is a summary of every change made:

---

## Em-dash (`---`) replacements

**abstract.tex (line 3):**
- `jailbreaks---attacks that` → `jailbreaks, i.e., attacks that`
- `variants---differing solely in their pretraining objective---share` → `variants (differing solely in their pretraining objective) share`
- `detection---the classification objective's structural role` → `detection, and the classification objective's structural role`

**introduction.tex (line 16):**
- `the classification objective's structural role---not label semantics---drives` → `the classification objective's structural role, not label semantics, drives`

**introduction.tex (line 20):**
- `benign-side factors---the first controlled` → `benign-side factors, constituting the first controlled`

**experiments.tex (line 117):**
- `the objective's structural role---partitioning the representation space---matters` → `the structural role of the objective (partitioning the representation space) matters` (also resolves the near-verbatim duplication with introduction.tex by rephrasing)

**conclusion.tex (line 25):**
- `0.596)---condition-specific, counterproductive` → `0.596), making these advantages condition-specific and counterproductive`

**related_work.tex (line 6):**
- `differently---gradual escalation` → `differently: gradual escalation`

## Participial `-ing` phrase fixes

**abstract.tex (line 3):**
- `indicating the classification objective's structural role` → replaced with `and the classification objective's structural role` (restructured clause)

**experiments.tex (line 59):**
- `confirming domain-adapted representations capture` → `that confirms domain-adapted representations capture`

**experiments.tex (line 131):**
- `confirming detection relies on per-turn content` → `; detection therefore relies on per-turn content`

**experiments.tex (line 167):**
- `revealing a difficulty threshold:` → split into new sentence: `This exposes a difficulty threshold:`

**experiments.tex (line 203):**
- `confirming transfer to human-written attacks` → `which confirms transfer to human-written attacks`

**conclusion.tex (line 15):**
- `localizing to 3 SVD dimensions` → `concentrated in 3 SVD dimensions`

All en-dashes (`--`) for numeric ranges were preserved. `appendix.tex` was not touched.
```

```json
    "snippet": "1\t% experiments.tex\n2\t\n3\t\\section{Experiments}\n4\t\n5\t\\paragraph{Terminology.}\n6\t\\emph{Cross-attack OOD}: evaluation on attack families unseen during training; benign distribution remains in-distribution (IID) throughout (OOD reflects attack-side shift only). $K$: prefix length (number of conversation turns retained). \\emph{DAPT}: all Stage-1 adaptations; we extend \\citet{gururangan2020dontstop} to supervised variants. We refer to the performance ranking of DAPT variants across OOD conditions as the \\emph{evidence hierarchy} (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}).\n7\t\n8\t\\subsection{Setup}\n9\t\n10\t\\paragraph{Data.}\n11\tAll synthetic data is generated by Qwen3-8B~\\citep{qwen3_2025}.\n12\tThe IID dataset contains 250 Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo} jailbreak and 250 benign conversations, split into training (350), validation (74), and test (76) with balanced classes.\n13\tStage~1 uses training + validation; Stage~2 trains on training, early-stops on validation.\n14\tOOD evaluation pairs 80 attack-specific conversations with 38 IID-test benign: FITD~\\citep{fitd2024}, DD~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024}, and ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} (118 total each; no template overlap across families).\n15\tA paraphrased IID test set (BERTScore 0.92) serves as the robustness benchmark (Section~\\ref{sec:perturbation}).\n16\tFor human validation, we use ToxicChat~\\citep{lin2023toxicchat} (204+204 from real LMSYS interactions).\n17\t\n18\t\\paragraph{Models.}\n19\tSeven DeBERTa-v3 variants share the same Stage-2 pipeline and differ only in Stage-1 training (Section~\\ref{sec:method}).\n20\tOur vanilla DeBERTa+BiGRU configuration is architecturally equivalent to DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026} and differs only in encoder choice (DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\\ BERT); it serves as our primary baseline.\n21\tFor cross-encoder validation, we replicate vanilla, MLM, and 9-class variants on RoBERTa-base~\\citep{liu2019roberta} (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_encoder}).\n22\tWe also compare TF-IDF (term frequency--inverse document frequency) logistic regression, LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}), and Llama Guard~3~8B~\\citep{metallamaguard3_2024} (zero-shot; evaluates last-response safety given conversation context), which detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks (5/278 pooled recall; Table~\\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}).\n23\t\n24\t\\paragraph{Metrics.}\n25\tF1 for binary jailbreak detection at prefix $K$=2 and full conversations (full per-$K$ in Appendix~\\ref{sec:full_prefix}).\n26\tOOD results report 3-seed mean$\\pm$std (seeds 42, 123, 456).\n27\tFor pairwise comparisons, we report bias-corrected and accelerated (BCa) bootstrap 95\\% confidence intervals (10,000 resamples; assumes cross-seed independence) and paired permutation tests; seed-level values confirm all conclusions hold at every seed (Appendix~\\ref{app:seed_level}).\n28\t\n29\t\\paragraph{Training.}\n30\tStage-1: learning rate $2 \\times 10^{-5}$, batch 16, 4 epochs, MLM masking 15\\%.\n31\tStage-2: two-layer MLP, 20 epochs.\n32\t\n33\t%----------------------------------------------------------------------\n34\t\\subsection{Cross-Attack Generalization}\n35\t\n36\t\\begin{table}[t]\n37\t\\centering\n38\t\\small\n39\t\\caption{Cross-attack generalization (F1) at $K$=2 and full conversation length. IID: Crescendo; OOD: Deceptive Delight (DD). 3-seed mean$\\pm$std where noted; full per-$K$ in Appendix~\\ref{sec:full_prefix}.}\n40\t\\label{tab:main_results}\n41\t\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n42\t\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc|cc@{}}\n43\t\\toprule\n44\t& \\multicolumn{2}{c|}{\\textit{IID (Cresc.)}} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textit{OOD (DD)}} \\\\\n45\t\\textbf{Method} & $K$=2 & Full & $K$=2 & Full \\\\\n46\t\\midrule\n47\t9-class & \\textbf{1.000$\\pm$.000} & \\textbf{1.000$\\pm$.000} & .981$\\pm$.013 & \\textbf{.997$\\pm$.005} \\\\\n48\tMLM-only$^{\\ddagger}$ & .974 & 1.000 & \\textbf{.993$\\pm$.009} & .914$\\pm$.115 \\\\\n49\tVanilla & .978$\\pm$.016 & 1.000$\\pm$.000 & .479$\\pm$.038 & .312$\\pm$.026 \\\\\n50\tTF-IDF LR$^{\\S}$ & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & .961 \\\\\n51\t\\bottomrule\n52\t\\end{tabular}}\n53\t\\vspace{-2pt}\n54\t{\\footnotesize $^{\\ddagger}$MLM-only IID reports single seed (42); OOD reports 3-seed. $^{\\S}$TF-IDF is deterministic (no std).}\n55\t\\end{table}\n56\t\n57\tVanilla encoders collapse under cross-attack transfer; domain-adapted variants recover near-perfect detection (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}, Figure~\\ref{fig:cross_attack_bar}).\n58\tOn OOD DD, vanilla drops to F1\\,=\\,0.312$\\pm$0.026 and worsens with more turns (0.628$\\to$0.312 at Full), consistent with attack turns occupying benign embedding regions~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}. MLM recovers to $\\geq$0.97 at $K \\leq 5$ without labels but is unstable at full length (0.914$\\pm$0.115); 9-class maintains 0.997$\\pm$0.005.\n59\tOn strategy-diverse ActorAttack, TF-IDF collapses (0.261) while 9-class maintains 0.972$\\pm$0.026, a 71 percentage point (pp) gap that confirms domain-adapted representations capture beyond-lexical features.\n60\tSeed-level results confirm consistent separation (Table~\\ref{tab:seed_level}; bootstrap CIs non-overlapping, permutation $p < 0.0001$). FITD is non-discriminative (all F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.990).\n61\t\n62\t\\begin{figure}[t]\n63\t\\centering\n64\t\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig2_cross_attack_bar.pdf}\n65\t\\caption{Cross-attack overview (F1 at full conversation length). DisPeD (9-class) maintains near-perfect detection across IID and both OOD families; vanilla collapses on OOD DD. FITD is non-discriminative for all methods.}\n66\t\\label{fig:cross_attack_bar}\n67\t\\end{figure}\n68\t\n69\tPrefix-length analysis (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:full_prefix}) confirms 9-class stability across all prefixes (0.972--0.997) vs.\\ MLM instability at full length (0.914$\\pm$0.115) and vanilla degradation (0.628$\\to$0.312).\n70\t\n71\t%----------------------------------------------------------------------\n72\t\\subsection{Decomposing the Detection Mechanism}\n73\t\\label{sec:decomposition}\n74\t\n75\t\\begin{figure}[t]\n76\t\\centering\n77\t\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig3_evidence_hierarchy.pdf}\n78\t\\caption{Evidence hierarchy across evaluation conditions. Classification-based DAPT outperforms MLM and vanilla on synthetic OOD attacks (DD, AA), but the hierarchy reverses on human-authored single-turn data (ToxicChat), where JB-MLM leads.}\n79\t\\label{fig:evidence_hierarchy}\n80\t\\end{figure}\n81\t\n82\t\\begin{table}[t]\n83\t\\centering\n84\t\\small\n85\t\\caption{Evidence hierarchy on OOD DD and ActorAttack (AA) at full conversation length (3-seed mean$\\pm$std where available). OOD F1 reflects attack-side generalization only; benign samples are IID from training distribution (benign-side shift evaluated in Section~\\ref{sec:benign_shift}). Bold indicates best per column. All variants share the same Stage-2 pipeline. Full per-prefix results in Appendix Tables~\\ref{tab:full_prefix} and~\\ref{tab:actorattack_full}. Bottom rows: full dual-OOD evaluation with WildChat benign replacing IID benign (Appendix~\\ref{app:dual_ood}).}\n86\t\\label{tab:evidence_hierarchy}\n87\t\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n88\t\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n89\t\\toprule\n90\t\\textbf{Variant} & DD-Full & AA-Full & DD-$K$=2 \\\\\n91\t\\midrule\n92\t9-class (full model) & .997$\\pm$.005 & .972$\\pm$.026 & .981$\\pm$.013 \\\\\n93\tBinary (2-class) & \\textbf{1.000$\\pm$.000} & \\textbf{.990$\\pm$.008} & .994$\\pm$.009 \\\\\n94\tScrambled labels & .997$\\pm$.005 & .951$\\pm$.043 & .981$\\pm$.026 \\\\\n95\tMLM-only$^{\\ddagger}$ (no labels) & .914$\\pm$.115 & .893$\\pm$.080 & .993$\\pm$.009 \\\\\n96\tMean pooling$^{*}$ & .991$\\pm$.013 & .962$\\pm$.015 & .994$\\pm$.009 \\\\\n97\t\\midrule\n98\tTopic classif.\\ (5-class) & .541$\\pm$.207 & .625$\\pm$.141 & .593$\\pm$.166 \\\\\n99\tWikipedia MLM & .904$\\pm$.115 & .686$\\pm$.297 & .911$\\pm$.073 \\\\\n100\tVanilla DeBERTa & .312$\\pm$.026 & .258$\\pm$.000 & .479$\\pm$.038 \\\\\n101\tTF-IDF LR$^{\\dagger}$ & .961 & .261 & \\textbf{1.000} \\\\\n102\tLlama Guard~3$^{\\P}$ & .024 & .000 & -- \\\\\n103\t\\midrule\n104\t\\multicolumn{4}{l}{\\textit{Dual-OOD (WildChat benign; Section~\\ref{sec:benign_shift})}} \\\\[2pt]\n105\t9-class (no adapt) & .855 & .842 & -- \\\\\n106\t9-class (+10-shot) & .994 & .981 & -- \\\\\n107\t\\bottomrule\n108\t\\end{tabular}}\n109\t\\vspace{-2pt}\n\n... {
```
```json
  "parentUuid": "cd7abfd6-cad4-4370-b22a-a08efee76c55",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "edited_text_file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "snippet": "1\t% introduction.tex\n2\t\n3\t\\section{Introduction}\n4\t\n5\tMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.\n6\t\n7\tExisting defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \\emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?} Pretrained language understanding alone does not suffice: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\\citep{he2023debertav3} with temporal aggregation (cf.\\ DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}) drops from F1\\,=\\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 (across prefix lengths) with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization at base model scale benefits substantially from domain adaptation.\n8\t\n9\t\\begin{figure*}[t]\n10\t\\centering\n11\t\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n12\t\\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}\n13\t\\label{fig:architecture}\n14\t\\end{figure*}\n15\t\n16\tOur pipeline, \\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection)\\footnote{We use `disentangled' to describe our experimental methodology of isolating individual factors through controlled ablation, distinct from disentangled representation learning ($\\beta$-VAE, InfoGAN).}, separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants each isolate a single factor while holding all others constant (Section~\\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline. Controlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families reveals three findings at base model scale: classification-based domain-adaptive pretraining (DAPT) is the only variant that consistently recovers OOD detection (F1\\,=\\,0.97--1.0 vs.\\ vanilla 0.26--0.31), even under mixed-LLM training where unsupervised pretraining collapses to vanilla level~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}; scrambled labels match correct labels for template attacks, which shows that the classification objective's structural role, not label semantics, drives generalization; yet this hierarchy reverses entirely on human-authored data (MLM 0.837 vs.\\ classification 0.596), revealing condition-specific rather than universal advantages. At 355M parameters, capacity alone suffices: RoBERTa-large handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation.\n17\t\n18\tOur contributions:\n19\t\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n20\t  \\item \\textbf{Systematic ablation of cross-attack generalization.} Thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125--355M) decompose jailbreak detection into attack-side and benign-side factors, constituting the first controlled factorial study in this domain. Classification DAPT recovers OOD detection (0.26--0.31 $\\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0) while unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training; 10--25 benign conversations reduce false-positive rates (FPR) from 58--68\\% to 0--1.5\\%.\n21\t\n22\t  \\item \\textbf{Partition sufficiency with a difficulty gradient.} Scrambled labels match correct labels for template-attack detection (Two One-Sided Tests [TOST] $|d| \\leq 0.14$), but strategy-diverse attacks retain moderate label sensitivity ($d$\\,=\\,0.44). Domain-relevant partitioning---not label semantics---is the operative mechanism, consistent with \\citet{zhang2021understanding}: compression localizes to 3 singular value decomposition (SVD) dimensions in layers 8--12.\n23\t\n24\t  \\item \\textbf{Practical adaptation protocol.} A 184M-parameter encoder achieves 24.3$\\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (43$\\times$ fewer parameters; Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}); few-shot benign adaptation resolves deployment-blocking FPR without target-domain attack data.\n25\t\\end{itemize}"
  },
```

```text
=== abstract.tex ===
(none)
=== introduction.tex ===
22:  \item \textbf{Partition sufficiency with a difficulty gradient.} Scrambled labels match correct labels for template-attack detection (Two One-Sided Tests [TOST] $|d| \leq 0.14$), but strategy-diverse attacks retain moderate label sensitivity ($d$\,=\,0.44). Domain-relevant partitioning---not label semantics---is the operative mechanism, consistent with \citet{zhang2021understanding}: compression localizes to 3 singular value decomposition (SVD) dimensions in layers 8--12.
=== method.tex ===
(none)
=== experiments.tex ===
33:%----------------------------------------------------------------------
71:%----------------------------------------------------------------------
133:%----------------------------------------------------------------------
169:%----------------------------------------------------------------------
176:%----------------------------------------------------------------------
208:\caption{Condition-specific evidence hierarchy. On synthetic OOD (DD), classification DAPT dominates; on human-authored ToxicChat, unsupervised MLM leads---rank positions reverse entirely.}
219:%----------------------------------------------------------------------
225:%----------------------------------------------------------------------
=== conclusion.tex ===
21:Our findings suggest a capacity-aware deployment strategy. At base scale (125--184M), any domain-relevant classification objective recovers OOD detection---scrambled labels perform equivalently on template attacks, so practitioners need not invest in annotation quality for this regime. When facing strategy-diverse attacks (e.g., ActorAttack), the label effect resurfaces ($d$\,=\,0.44), favoring semantically meaningful taxonomies. At sufficient capacity ($\geq$355M), DAPT becomes unnecessary and potentially counterproductive; the decision to adapt should be conditioned on encoder scale. Independent of adaptation choice, few-shot benign calibration (10--25 conversations per deployment domain) resolves false-positive rates without requiring target-domain attack data, enabling deployment across platforms with minimal data collection. The 184M-parameter pipeline maintains 24.3$\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge alternatives (43$\times$ fewer parameters; Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), making it suitable for real-time monitoring at scale.
=== related_work.tex ===
6:Multi-turn jailbreak families share a persuasion substrate~\citep{zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,li2024mhj} but operationalize it differently: gradual escalation~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, commitment-consistency bias~\citep{fitd2024}, emotional manipulation~\citep{deceptivedelight2024}, persona steering~\citep{ren2024actorattack}, context poisoning~\citep{alobaid2026echochamber}---while automated methods~\citep{chao2023pair,mehrotra2024tap} iteratively refine adversarial prompts~\citep{perez2022redteaming}. Taxonomies~\citep{wei2023jailbroken} and benchmarks~\citep{mazeika2024harmbench,chao2024jailbreakbench,jiang2024redqueen,song2026multibreak} standardize evaluation. This shared substrate with diverse surface strategies defines our setting: a detector must generalize across families, not merely succeed on its training distribution.
```

```json
    "newString": "Domain-relevant partitioning, not label semantics, is the operative mechanism",
    "originalFile": "% introduction.tex\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.\n\nExisting defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \\emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?} Pretrained language understanding alone does not suffice: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\\citep{he2023debertav3} with temporal aggregation (cf.\\ DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}) drops from F1\\,=\\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 (across prefix lengths) with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization at base model scale benefits substantially from domain adaptation.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n\\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}\n\\label{fig:architecture}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nOur pipeline, \\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection)\\footnote{We use `disentangled' to describe our experimental methodology of isolating individual factors through controlled ablation, distinct from disentangled representation learning ($\\beta$-VAE, InfoGAN).}, separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants each isolate a single factor while holding all others constant (Section~\\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline. Controlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families reveals three findings at base model scale: classification-based domain-adaptive pretraining (DAPT) is the only variant that consistently recovers OOD detection (F1\\,=\\,0.97--1.0 vs.\\ vanilla 0.26--0.31), even under mixed-LLM training where unsupervised pretraining collapses to vanilla level~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}; scrambled labels match correct labels for template attacks, which shows that the classification objective's structural role, not label semantics, drives generalization; yet this hierarchy reverses entirely on human-authored data (MLM 0.837 vs.\\ classification 0.596), revealing condition-specific rather than universal advantages. At 355M parameters, capacity alone suffices: RoBERTa-large handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Systematic ablation of cross-attack generalization.} Thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125--355M) decompose jailbreak detection into attack-side and benign-side factors, constituting the first controlled factorial study in this domain. Classification DAPT recovers OOD detection (0.26--0.31 $\\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0) while unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training; 10--25 benign conversations reduce false-positive rates (FPR) from 58--68\\% to 0--1.5\\%.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Partition sufficiency with a difficulty gradient.} Scrambled labels match correct labels for template-attack detection (Two One-Sided Tests [TOST] $|d| \\leq 0.14$), but strategy-diverse attacks retain moderate label sensitivity ($d$\\,=\\,0.44). Domain-relevant partitioning---not label semantics---is the operative mechanism, consistent with \\citet{zhang2021understanding}: compression localizes to 3 singular value decomposition (SVD) dimensions in layers 8--12.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Practical adaptation protocol.} A 184M-parameter encoder achieves 24.3$\\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (43$\\times$ fewer parameters; Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}); few-shot benign adaptation resolves deployment-blocking FPR without target-domain attack data.\n\\end{itemize}\n",
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QmRTE7Qs3cVzMPztVm6qHm`</summary>

```text
19	
20	\paragraph{Deployment implications.}
21	Our findings suggest a capacity-aware deployment strategy. At base scale (125--184M), any domain-relevant classification objective recovers OOD detection---scrambled labels perform equivalently on template attacks, so practitioners need not invest in annotation quality for this regime. When facing strategy-diverse attacks (e.g., ActorAttack), the label effect resurfaces ($d$\,=\,0.44), favoring semantically meaningful taxonomies. At sufficient capacity ($\geq$355M), DAPT becomes unnecessary and potentially counterproductive; the decision to adapt should be conditioned on encoder scale. Independent of adaptation choice, few-shot benign calibration (10--25 conversations per deployment domain) resolves false-positive rates without requiring target-domain attack data, enabling deployment across platforms with minimal data collection. The 184M-parameter pipeline maintains 24.3$\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge alternatives (43$\times$ fewer parameters; Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), making it suitable for real-time monitoring at scale.
22	
```

On single-turn human-authored data the hierarchy reverses (MLM leads; Appendix~\\ref{app:confidence}); truncated analysis localizes the reversal to distributional gap rather than detection mechanism (Appendix~\\ref{app:truncated}). Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains 0.818 while MLM collapses; at 355M, DAPT becomes counterproductive (vanilla AA\\,=\\,1.000 vs.\\ 9-class 0.946). WildChat FPR reaches 68.1\\% (synthetic-to-real shift). Length-matched evaluation ($N$=155) shows negligible DD change ($\\Delta$\\,=\\,$-$2.5\\,pp; Appendices~\\ref{app:length_independence},~\\ref{app:length}); ActorAttack padding collapse ($-$57.9\\,pp) reflects signal dilution (Appendix~\\ref{app:truncated}).\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment implications.}\nOur findings suggest a capacity-aware deployment strategy. At base scale (125--184M), any domain-relevant classification objective recovers OOD detection---scrambled labels perform equivalently on template attacks, so practitioners need not invest in annotation quality for this regime. When facing strategy-diverse attacks (e.g., ActorAttack), the label effect resurfaces ($d$\\,=\\,0.44), favoring semantically meaningful taxonomies. At sufficient capacity ($\\geq$355M), DAPT becomes unnecessary and potentially counterproductive; the decision to adapt should be conditioned on encoder scale. Independent of adaptation choice, few-shot benign calibration (10--25 conversations per deployment domain) resolves false-positive rates without requiring target-domain attack data, enabling deployment across platforms with minimal data collection. The 184M-parameter pipeline maintains 24.3$\\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge alternatives (43$\\times$ fewer parameters; Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), making it suitable for real-time monitoring at scale.\n\n\\section{Conclusion}\n\nClassification DAPT recovers OOD detection at base scale (F1\\,=\\,0.97--1.0 vs.\\ vanilla 0.26--0.31), though not under mixed-LLM training on diverse attacks (ActorAttack F1\\,=\\,0.504); on single-turn human-authored data, the hierarchy reverses (ToxicChat MLM 0.837 vs.\\ classification 0.596), making these advantages condition-specific and counterproductive at 355M. Few-shot adaptation reduces FPR from 58--68\\% to 0--1.5\\%; this ablation framework provides a template for future controlled studies of domain adaptation in safety-critical natural language processing (NLP) tasks.\n\n\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Synthetic data.} All findings derive from synthetic conversations (single LLM, Qwen3-8B; 350 training, 3 seeds); the hierarchy reverses on human single-turn data (ToxicChat), and no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists.\n\\item \\textbf{IID benign assumption.} OOD F1 assumes IID benign data; few-shot adaptation recovers performance but is validated on only two platforms ($n \\leq 50$).\n\\item \\textbf{Scope.} Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks at base-to-large scale (125--355M); single-turn token-level attacks and non-text modalities are not addressed.\n\\end{itemize}\n",

```text
5	\paragraph{Multi-turn jailbreak attacks.}
6	Multi-turn jailbreak families share a persuasion substrate~\citep{zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,li2024mhj} but operationalize it differently: gradual escalation~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, commitment-consistency bias~\citep{fitd2024}, emotional manipulation~\citep{deceptivedelight2024}, persona steering~\citep{ren2024actorattack}, context poisoning~\citep{alobaid2026echochamber}---while automated methods~\citep{chao2023pair,mehrotra2024tap} iteratively refine adversarial prompts~\citep{perez2022redteaming}. Taxonomies~\citep{wei2023jailbroken} and benchmarks~\citep{mazeika2024harmbench,chao2024jailbreakbench,jiang2024redqueen,song2026multibreak} standardize evaluation. This shared substrate with diverse surface strategies defines our setting: a detector must generalize across families, not merely succeed on its training distribution.
7	
```

```json
    "originalFile": "% related_work.tex\n\n\\section{Related Work}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn jailbreak attacks.}\nMulti-turn jailbreak families share a persuasion substrate~\\citep{zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,li2024mhj} but operationalize it differently: gradual escalation~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, commitment-consistency bias~\\citep{fitd2024}, emotional manipulation~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024}, persona steering~\\citep{ren2024actorattack}, context poisoning~\\citep{alobaid2026echochamber}---while automated methods~\\citep{chao2023pair,mehrotra2024tap} iteratively refine adversarial prompts~\\citep{perez2022redteaming}. Taxonomies~\\citep{wei2023jailbroken} and benchmarks~\\citep{mazeika2024harmbench,chao2024jailbreakbench,jiang2024redqueen,song2026multibreak} standardize evaluation. This shared substrate with diverse surface strategies defines our setting: a detector must generalize across families, not merely succeed on its training distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn defenses and production safety.}\nNo existing defense evaluates cross-attack transfer. Recent conversation-level systems pair fine-tuned encoders with sequence aggregation~\\citep{deepcontext2026,tong2025biid,gguard2025,safedream2026}, yet each evaluates on a single attack family; absent public implementations preclude direct comparison (Appendix~\\ref{app:bert_proxy}). Representation-level approaches~\\citep{lu2025xboundary,zhang2025jbshield,xie2024gradsafe,zou2024circuitbreakers,wu2025safeint,shen2024jailbreakantidote,yousefpour2025repbend,kulkarni2026latentadversarial,kadali2025internallayers,kadali2026trace,hua2026rcs} require white-box access and can be brittle under adaptive attacks~\\citep{derya2026revisitingjbshield}. Per-turn classifiers~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard,metallamaguard3_2024,zeng2024shieldgemma,han2024wildguard,anthropic2025constitutional} and production systems~\\citep{markov2023holistic} cannot model multi-turn trajectories; perturbation-based~\\citep{robey2024smoothllm} and decoding-based~\\citep{xu2024safedecoding} defenses target single-turn attacks. Our ablation fills this gap: decomposition across thirteen configurations under OOD evaluation identifies domain exposure as the dominant factor behind cross-attack transfer.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational persuasion and discourse.}\nDiscourse and persuasion frameworks~\\citep{mann1988rst,prasad2008pdtb,stolcke2000dialogueact,austin1962speechacts,searle1969speechacts} offer interpretable vocabulary for attack strategies. Building on Cialdini's principles~\\citep{cialdini2001influence}, PAP~\\citep{zeng2024pap} identifies 40 persuasion techniques and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks; propaganda analysis~\\citep{dasanmartino2019fine,dasanmartino2020semeval} and persuasive dialogue~\\citep{wang2019persuasion} apply similar classification. Our ablation disentangles these roles: the taxonomy enables strategy characterization, but detection operates through domain-adapted embeddings regardless of label correctness.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain-adaptive pretraining.}\nContinued pretraining on domain text yields consistent gains~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop,lee2020biobert,beltagy2019scibert,howard2018ulmfit}, yet this has not been disentangled for safety-critical generalization. Intermediate-task transfer~\\citep{phang2018stilts} and multi-task learning~\\citep{caruana1997multitask,liu2019mtdnn} confound domain exposure with supervised signal; our MLM control isolates the contribution. Dataset artifact analysis~\\citep{gururangan2018artifacts,niven2019probing} motivates the TF-IDF baseline, adversarial NLP~\\citep{jin2020textfooler,li2020bertattack,nasr2025attackermovessecond,andriushchenko2024adaptive} informs the perturbation protocol, and probing~\\citep{conneau2018probing,hewitt2019structural} underpins CKA analysis.\n",

Taxonomies~\\citep{wei2023jailbroken} and benchmarks~\\citep{mazeika2024harmbench,chao2024jailbreakbench,jiang2024redqueen,song2026multibreak} standardize evaluation. This shared substrate with diverse surface strategies defines our setting: a detector must generalize across families, not merely succeed on its training distribution.",
          "+Multi-turn jailbreak families share a persuasion substrate~\\citep{zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,li2024mhj} but operationalize it differently: gradual escalation~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, commitment-consistency bias~\\citep{fitd2024}, emotional manipulation~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024}, persona steering~\\citep{ren2024actorattack}, context poisoning~\\citep{alobaid2026echochamber}. Automated methods~\\citep{chao2023pair,mehrotra2024tap} iteratively refine adversarial prompts~\\citep{perez2022redteaming}. Taxonomies~\\citep{wei2023jailbroken} and benchmarks~\\citep{mazeika2024harmbench,chao2024jailbreakbench,jiang2024redqueen,song2026multibreak} standardize evaluation. This shared substrate with diverse surface strategies defines our setting: a detector must generalize across families, not merely succeed on its training distribution.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Multi-turn defenses and production safety.}",
          " No existing defense evaluates cross-attack transfer. Recent conversation-level systems pair fine-tuned encoders with sequence aggregation~\\citep{deepcontext2026,tong2025biid,gguard2025,safedream2026}, yet each evaluates on a single attack family; absent public implementations preclude direct comparison (Appendix~\\ref{app:bert_proxy}). Representation-level approaches~\\citep{lu2025xboundary,zhang2025jbshield,xie2024gradsafe,zou2024circuitbreakers,wu2025safeint,shen2024jailbreakantidote,yousefpour2025repbend,kulkarni2026latentadversarial,kadali2025internallayers,kadali2026trace,hua2026rcs} require white-box access and can be brittle under adaptive attacks~\\citep{derya2026revisitingjbshield}. Per-turn classifiers~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard,metallamaguard3_2024,zeng2024shieldgemma,han2024wildguard,anthropic2025constitutional} and production systems~\\citep{markov2023holistic} cannot model multi-turn trajectories; perturbation-based~\\citep{robey2024smoothllm} and decoding-based~\\citep{xu2024safedecoding} defenses target single-turn attacks. Our ablation fills this gap: decomposition across thirteen configurations under OOD evaluation identifies domain exposure as the dominant factor behind cross-attack transfer."
        ]
      }
    ],
```

_source line 3366 · 2026-05-25T05:27:36.315Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01CcJeMBT6kaBsQzoZ1m6sAD`</summary>

```text
203:\section{Cross-LLM Validation}
204:\label{app:cross_llm}
209:\caption{Cross-LLM validation (F1, full conversation). All models are trained on Qwen3-8B data; conditions vary the source of jailbreak (JB) and benign (ben.) conversations at inference. Values are evaluated under a matched 50-sample subset protocol and differ from Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}, which uses the full evaluation set.}
210:\label{tab:cross_llm}
227:Table~\ref{tab:cross_llm} shows three inference conditions. When both jailbreak and benign text come from Llama (row~2), all variants collapse (F1 = 0.26--0.43), but this conflates two sources of distribution shift: unfamiliar jailbreak patterns and unfamiliar benign text. Row~3 isolates jailbreak transferability by pairing Llama jailbreaks with the original Qwen benign distribution. Under this condition, 9-class recovers to F1 = 1.000 on DD and 0.801 on ActorAttack, and JB-MLM reaches 0.903 on ActorAttack. The contrast between rows~2 and~3 shows that the dominant failure mode in row~2 is benign distribution mismatch (false positives from unfamiliar benign text), not inability to recognize cross-LLM jailbreak patterns. Vanilla remains at 0.26--0.31 across all conditions, further confirming that encoders without DAPT lack OOD detection capability regardless of the LLM source. The residual degradation on ActorAttack (0.801 vs.\ 0.972) reflects genuine cross-LLM variation in structurally complex attack patterns.
232:\caption{Error decomposition for DD Full cross-LLM conditions (matched 50-sample subsets). TP rate is identical across benign conditions; the F1 collapse in the Llama-only condition (Table~\ref{tab:cross_llm}, row~2) is driven entirely by false positives on unfamiliar benign text.}
233:\label{tab:cross_llm_error}
245:Table~\ref{tab:cross_llm_error} decomposes DD Full F1 into true positive and false positive rates. TP rates are identical regardless of the benign source: domain-adapted models detect Llama-generated jailbreaks at the same rate whether evaluated against Qwen or Llama benign text. The F1 collapse in the full Llama condition (Table~\ref{tab:cross_llm}, row~2) is driven entirely by false positives on unfamiliar benign text (FP rate: 88--90\% on Llama vs.\ 0--0.9\% on Qwen for adapted variants). The model implicitly treats non-Qwen text style as a jailbreak indicator, a spurious correlation from single-LLM training data. This failure reflects a data diversity limitation, not a methodological one; mixed-LLM training (Table~\ref{tab:mixed_llm}) partially addresses this confound.
456:Table~\ref{tab:wildchat_fpr} evaluates false positive rates on 226 WildChat benign multi-turn conversations from real GPT-3.5 users (avg 7.4 turns). All variants produce FPR far exceeding deployable thresholds: 9-class is worst at 68.1\%, followed by vanilla (48.1\%) and MLM (44.5\%). Domain adaptation \emph{increases} FPR relative to vanilla (+20\,pp for 9-class), indicating that continued pretraining amplifies dependence on the training benign distribution. This is consistent with the cross-LLM false positive spike (Table~\ref{tab:cross_llm_error}): models trained on synthetic Qwen3-8B benign text implicitly treat unfamiliar conversational styles as jailbreak indicators. High MLM seed variance (std\,=\,14.3\%) suggests that unsupervised pretraining is particularly unstable under benign distribution shift. Deployment requires per-domain benign calibration or mixed-source benign training data.
```

</details>

[WRITE HANDOFF] 请立即写你的 handoff。

**格式硬约束**:
- 整条回复**只能**是 &lt;handoff>...&lt;/handoff> 标签包裹
- 禁止在标签外写任何寒暄 / 说明 / 确认（"好的"/"我开始写"/"写完了"等）

**必须覆盖以下 5 个 section（缺一不可，无内容写 N/A）**:

### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
  本项目特定的不可违反约束（GPU 设备号、服务器名、账号、路径限制等）。
  写本项目特定的，不要抄 CLAUDE.md 通用 L1 红线。
  下一个"你"第一眼看到的就是这里，写错了会被 Director CRITICAL。

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
  - 服务器名 + 工作目录 + Python 路径 + conda env
  - 模型路径 / 数据集路径 / wandb config / HF cache
  - 活跃进程：PID + tmux session + cron 任务 ID
  写一次就稳定，下一个"你"不用再 SSH 探查。

### 3. 📊 当前状态
  - 你刚做完什么 / 正在做什么
  - registry 里 RUNNING/PREPARING 的实验列表 + 一句话摘要
  - 等待中的 Worker 回调 / Cron / Director 决策

### 4. 📋 待办（条件触发式）
  格式 "条件 X 满足 → 动作 Y"。例如:
  - "Falcon-H1R 下载完成 → sed 修 MODEL_PATH → dry-run → 提交"
  - "pilot_011 出 GC4 结果 → ≥2pp 转架构 narrative，<2pp 写 null-result"

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
  - 项目核心结论 / 决策链（D001..）
  - 下一个"你"最容易判断错的事 / 容易踩的坑

**写作原则**：
- 第 1 节必须是项目特定的（不是通用 L1 红线）
- 第 2 节让下一轮 BOOT 后能立即开工，避免重新 SSH 探查
- 简洁 > 完整：每节 5-10 行足够，长 handoff 不等于好 handoff

示例格式:
&lt;handoff>
### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
- 本项目只能用 cuda:4 / cuda:5（之前两次违规 cuda:0 被 Director CRITICAL）
- submit_training_job 不支持 shell builtin（source/conda activate 全不行）

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
- 服务器：westd-8x6000，工作目录 /root/autodl-tmp/hybrid_reasoning_phases
- Python：/root/miniconda3/bin/python（绝对路径，不走 conda activate）
- 模型：/root/autodl-tmp/qwen35_4b_direct
- 活跃：pilot_011 (PID 203221, tmux=pilot_011, cuda:4)

### 3. 📊 当前状态
我刚完成 pilot_010 分析，正在等 pilot_011 (Falcon-H1R) CoT 生成完成（6/50）。
registry: pilot_011 RUNNING；其他全 DONE。

### 4. 📋 待办
- pilot_011 cron 回调 → spawn worker 跑 probe + GC4 计算
- GC4 ≥2pp → 转架构 narrative；<2pp → null-result paper outline 已就绪

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
Falcon-H1R 是最后一个数据点。已知 4B/9B GC4 都 FAIL。
